### **@TheSundanceKid-s9f** | 👍 17 likes
[View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_bXHBT5NTfc&lc=Ugxg7JwA2ztVmQvsVjR4AaABAg)

I wish to offer a recontextualization of Gnostic beliefs which align more closely to the concept of the Father's (Monad, Elyon, Buddha) infinite love.  I don't have much time, so I need to be somewhat brief; I apologize if I cause any confusion with my words.

First:  Perfection cannot gain Wisdom as that implies failure, since Wisdom is earned through the study of the effect of imperfection.  Therefor, there must be a source of imperfection from which the Father in its perfection can infinitely glean Wisdom; this is the result of the Father glimpsing itself, and contextualizing its reflection *as* a reflection of itself - an emanation, or a contextualized opposite, which resulted in the formation of the Father's Wisdom.  This is what we call Sofia, firmly conceptualized against the Father's divine masculine as the divine feminine.

Second:  To attain Wisdom, the Mother - Sofia - must Suffer.  To facilitate this, Sofia as a reflected emanation is apart from and opposite of the Father, and thus *encapsulates* the very concept of Suffering itself.  Sofia is functionally the darkness juxtaposed against the Father's light; infinity from both ends of the spectrum meeting at a centerpoint where the two may achieve a sort of divine synthesis.  To attain Wisdom, ideas - to which I refer as 'Templates' - from the Father are sent from on high to be brought low within Sophia, wherein they are elevated within her context as Wisdom before being sent back to the Father to be processed.  

To illustrate this process, simply draw an infinity symbol starting from the tallest point of the first circle.

It was in this act of experiential coitus - and yes, I'm being serious - where Yahweh was born.  Infinitely capable of both; a being of infinite potential... And infinite ego.  Yahweh was a jealous egotist who believed he stole from the Father and the Mother when he used the Father's Templates to create material reality.  His Old Testament character was one of a horrific LORD that saw to rule over Man.

To understand why this was Allowed, we have to understand the character of the Father.  The Father is, at heart, a creator; he builds, he tinkers, and above all, he *plans.*  His plans are perfect in that they are ineffable, not immutable; plans that have an ineffable end can be changed, but will always see their goal through regardless of alleged surprise.  This is why the creation of Yahweh can surprise Elyon, but only *pleasantly* so; this new variable was factored into an ineffable plan to teach Yahweh the dichotomy of the Father and the Mother, and thus teach him the dichotomy of Good and Evil, the purpose of Meaning, and the perfection of divine Harmony between the Father and its emanation.

This could only be achieved through Human Experience, which can only be contextualized... *against its opposite.*

Yahweh was allowed to rule over us for a long time as a spiritual thing, but soon was manipulated - tricked, by the machinations of the ineffable plan - into egotistically assuming human form, likely to try and understand why we complained no matter WHAT it seemed to do.  This channeling of theoretically infinite potential into a human form allowed Yahweh's ego to be filtered through the lens of human experience, or the 'Geist' as it's known.  This filter helped Yahweh contextualize why what it did was a bad thing, and by experiencing the true breadth of human Suffering, understand why it needed to fix what it made broken.

That great chastising - that great HUMBLING - was Yahweh's experience as the Christ Jesus.

It's why the Yahweh of the New Testament is so, so much different.  It's why Yahweh as Jesus told us he was the SON of God, not God himself, and always spoke of his Father.  It's why he had to die for Sin's sake, and in such a terrible, atrocious way.  It's why Yahweh has become *temperate, loving, and accepting.*

He's trying to emulate the Father to right all of his wrongs, and in doing so, attempt to teach humanity a better way the *right* way.  NOT with force, but with compassion, pity, mercy, and love.

> ​@niswr7319 Wow.. That’s dismissive at best.   
>   
> I’m a history buff, and one thing is consistent across civilizations humans have always been materially conscious. This isn’t cultural bias, it’s structural reality. The moment a society develops surplus, it begins to encode value into objects, symbols, and language.  
>   
> Even so-called “primitive” societies revered material possessions. Ancestral weapons, ornaments, tools, and burial goods were treated as sacred precisely because they embodied continuity, labor, and status.  
>   
> Take Germanic societies, for example.  
>   
> Archaeology tells us this clearly. Germanic burial sites from the Migration Period and earlier are rich with:  
>   
> Gold bracteates, Bronze and silver torcs, Weapon grave goods deliberately crafted beyond utility  
>   
> Hoarded coinage not meant for circulation but for prestige  
>   
>   
> These weren’t accidental. They were symbols of honor, lineage, and divine favor.   
>   
> Even this very language reflects this. Phrases evolved around wealth as virtue-adjacent, not shameful:  
>   
> Wealth as means to uphold honor  
>   
> Prosperity as evidence of competence  
>   
> Poverty as misfortune, not holiness by default. So the idea that material awareness equals moral corruption is a modern propaganda, not a historical norm. As for China? reducing it to “money worship” you ignores Taoism, Confucian restraint, and Buddhist detachment entirely. Zen itself didn’t originate in Japan it came from mainland China (Chan Buddhism), later refined culturally in Japan. That alone undermines the claim of some uniquely Chinese material bias.

### **@Wojakio** | 👍 91 likes
[View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_bXHBT5NTfc&lc=Ugw602pP16pwg8x6ELd4AaABAg)

45年前我出生于中国。和大多数生长在共产主义国家的人一样，我从未了解、也从未想去了解属灵的世界。耶稣、上帝、灵魂、地狱和天堂。。。这些概念离得我是如此遥远，就算偶尔听到有人提及它们，我也只当是在听有关外星人、飞碟等事一般，认为和自己没有半点关系。学校给我们的教育是 - 宗教是鸦片，人没有灵魂，我们都只是由盐和矿物质所组成。人死后一了百了，什么上帝、耶稣、末日、天堂和地狱，统统是胡说八道。

我13岁的时候随父母移民到了美国，很快就有传道人向我传福音，试图让我认识耶稣基督。可我对他们所谈论的事半点也不感兴趣。记得在高中时，有一位名叫Natasha的同学向我传福音。虽然我没有直接说出来，可当时我内心的反应是：“这些人真是吃饱了没事干！怎么老是讲这些乱七八糟、无关紧要的东西？”。当这个同学问我：“每个人都有信仰。你的信仰是什么？”时，我还自以为是地回答：“我相信自己。”当时我还为这个“睿智”的回答沾沾自喜，现在回想起来连自己都脸红。那个我是多么的狂妄、愚蠢和无知。

在大学第一年，我认识了Juan Rojas，一位新皈依基督的同学。和每一个新认识耶稣的人一样，当时Juan的内心火热，逢人就说耶稣有多么好，给了自己莫大的恩典。Juan不止一次地邀请我和他一起查经学习。我可以看出Juan与其他人不同。他曾公开地向所有人坦白自己的罪行，连有些涉及自己隐私、很不堪的往事，他都会主动公布于众，其目的是让我们都知道，赦免他罪的耶稣是一位充满了恩典和慈爱的神。因此，尽管我不信他所信的，可他开诚布公的态度给我留下了深刻印象，所以我和他一起学习了几次圣经。不用说，我当时的内心根本不肯接受上帝的真理，所以参加了两次查经后就没有学下去了。

上帝却从未放弃过我。大学毕业后，我在德克萨斯州的一家公司里找到了一份体面的工作。我认识了许多新朋友，而他们当中很多人是基督徒。我记得当时自己时常会想：这些人真酷，可怎么他们都信什么基督教？实在是不能理解。在德州生活和工作的两年半里，我“过得很好”。我年轻且单身，收入也不错，还有很多空闲时间来让自己享受“自由”和罪给感官带来的快感。当然，那个时候我并不认为那样的生活方式有什么问题。如同许多无神论者喜欢用的辩解：“我又没伤害任何人，我没罪。”我只想满足肉体的欲望，所以我根本不知道什么是罪，也从不考虑什么是对、什么是错。“让我们吃喝吧！因为明天我们就死了。”

2004年我被公司解雇，不得不搬回家与父母同住。在接下来的三年里，我大部分时间都在失业状态里，没有收入、没有社交，整天抽烟、酗酒、赌博 … 我的生活看不到任何希望，精神陷入了深深的抑郁之中。虽然我从来没有想过要尝试，可脑海里时不时会浮现出自杀的念头。那是我的人生中一段至暗的日子。

2008年我28岁，由于一次偶然的事件，让我体验了一次类似于濒临死亡的经历。当时我的灵魂离开了躯体，进入了永恒。在那一刻我才知道，人不仅仅有一个必朽的肉体，还有一个永恒的灵魂。就如同电脑和手机有软、硬件，人也一样。人的硬件是我们在时间和空间里的肉体，软件则是我们的意识、思维和记忆，以及游离于时空之外的灵魂。我们肉体将随着时间消亡，而我们的灵魂将永远存在。所以没人希望自己的灵魂在永恒里受到半点折磨，因为任何延长到永恒的不适都是不堪忍受的。

这次经历深深改变了我的人生观。我下决心要去尝试新的事物，接触自己从未接触过的人。在做出这个决定的第二天，两名街头传道人敲响了我家的门，邀请我去参加他们周六的敬拜。此前出于寻求真理的目的，我也曾多次去过教堂，但每一次我在教堂里所遇到的负面、消极的经历，使我得出结论 - 就算上帝真实存在，祂也不不可能是在教堂里。但由于前一天我刚下决心去尝试新的事物，所以那天我孤身一人去参加了他们的聚会。那个周六早上，在和一群陌生人共同的敬拜里，我感受到了上帝的爱。自那时起我开始研习圣经，以及那个教会印的一本关于福音的小册子，里面的内容试图解答那些困扰人类的“终极问题” - 我是谁？生命的意义何在？我们从哪里来，又要往哪里去？

自那时起，我开始每周跟一位教会成员学习圣经。有一天晚上，忘了当时我是否在读经，突然我意识到自己是多么的邪恶。我一生中所做过的很多卑鄙往事，都一一呈现在我脑海中。我确信假如自己的躯体（硬件）在那一刻死去，我的灵魂（软件）将会直接站在造物主的跟前，接受祂公正的审判。我从圣经里认识了上帝的本性，我清楚自己当时的本性与造物主是截然相反的。上帝全然圣洁，我的灵魂肮脏不堪；上帝是美好的，我的灵魂沾满了罪恶。上帝的本质与我的本质水火不相容。因此，一旦我靠近上帝，我的灵魂必将被彻底摧毁，因为祂是一位全然圣洁的神，祂绝不允许任何污秽玷污自己，也绝不允许半点罪进入祂的荣耀。那时我的灵魂将永远离开上帝，落入永恒的咒诅，也就是所谓的下地狱。

于是在那一刻，我有了一辈子里所想过的最恐怖的念头。出于之前我对永恒的体验，我知道任何稍微一点的不适，在永恒里都将被无限放大，变得不可忍受。更别说永远在地狱里忍受那些难以想象、无穷无尽的折磨了。想着死后要下地狱，我陷入了极端的恐惧，觉得自己已无路可走。我只道自己没救了，在我肉体消亡的那一刻，灵魂也将落入地狱。就在那天晚上，我第一次跪下，向上帝祈祷。我呼求祂帮我，恳求祂原谅我犯下的所有罪孽。

在随后的两天里，我陷入了深深的抑郁。除了感到绝望外，我实在不知道自己应该做怎么做，才能挽救自己必下地狱的命运。这就好比一个人在刚出生时，他的生命纯洁得就如同一张完美的白纸。而他一生中所犯的罪，就像是在那张纯白纸上涂抹了黑色墨水，再将其揉成一团。从那以后，不论这个人如何努力地想把纸抹平，那怕是把它给烧了，都不可能去除上面的污渍和褶皱，让纸再变得洁白无痕了。我意识到自己的灵魂就像那个沾满污秽的纸团，在那一刻，我多么希望上帝可以让时光倒流，使我回到生命还是一张白纸的时候。唯有那样，我才能不去犯那些罪，才能弥补自己对上帝的亏欠。当然时光机是不存在的。

有天晚上我在读圣经，来到了约翰福音3:16：“神爱世人，甚至将他的独生子赐给他们，叫一切信他的不致灭亡，反得永生。”当我读这节经文时，脑海里突然蹦出了一个想法 - 噢，天呐，这正是上帝给我的答案呀。上帝不需要将时间拨回到我们还没有犯罪的时候，祂给了我们另一条路，让我们和祂重归于好，在祂面前变得完整无缺。这条路也可使我们早已被揉成一团、沾满污渍的灵魂，变得纯净无暇 - 只要我们相信上帝唯一的儿子耶稣基督，上帝不但会赦免我们的罪，祂还会赐给我们永恒的生命。

那是我这辈子听过的最好的消息！

当我意识到耶稣基督是救赎时，我感觉自己像是个溺水之人，突然见到身旁漂来一根大木头。出于本能，我拼命地抓住那根木头，无论如何也不松手。我认定自己与上帝的关系已被修复到完好如初，我的灵魂可以坦然无惧地站在上帝的面前，接受祂永恒的审判。

Eastern and western alchemy are congruent across the board. Professor Jiang, I'd like to share a perspective on Jesus’ teachings regarding the 'poor.' In this context, 'poor' refers to those attached to material things or burdened by the anxieties of the world, rather than just those lacking money. For instance, when Jesus filled the fishermen's nets, He first relieved them of their debt and scarcity mindset. Only after their material fear was gone were they truly free to detach from the world and follow a higher purpose.

> @higginswallop5009 Thank you for being respectful and this is a great reply. I’d like to challenge some of what you said:

> even with context of the time, the culture, and what is included in the book of the quote (literary context), the meaning of hypostasis is pretty clear in the Bible. “the exact imprint of his nature” (Heb. 11:3) This is the first chapter of this section. The first 3 verses spell out the definition and how it applies. Verse 1  explains throughout time, “God spoke to our fathers by the prophets,” (Heb. 11:1) God spoke to our ancestors through other men in the past. The second verse sets it up: in these days last, He has “spoken to us by his Son, whom he appointed the heir of all things,” through Him he also created the world. (Heb. 1:2). Without going into Verse 3, already we see the literary context. In short: God spoke to us in recent days through His Son and through His Son He created the world. The Son has all things as appointed to by the Father. So if after it is established that the Son, Who is Jesus Christ the great teacher and once man, has all things and has aided in creating this world, it can be assumed that Jesus is at very least supernatural. More than just a man. One who existed billions of years before us, yet existed on Earth: we know Him as a man. It seems like there’s two beings or natures present in one identifying person (the Son). A Supernatural Nature and a Human Nature. Supernatural because He existed before the world was created and actually through Him it was created. Human because he’s a historical figure known for being the best teacher to ever live. Verse 3 of course seals the deal on what the Supernatural Nature really is: “the exact imprint of his nature” (Heb. 1:3). I believe some translations have it as “substance” but either way, it very clearly shows that this supernatural nature is quite literally the exact imprint of God’s nature. Which means He has the nature of God. And he existed as a human so he definitely has human nature. Two natures coexisting. It has to be the same person yet two natures because God can never cease to exist. When the Son became man, the God nature didn’t extinguish. Yet a human nature was added. So if the God nature stayed, and the human nature was added…then it is two natures coexisting: hypostasis.   
>   
> Deductions from Scripture are necessary and have been present in the culture of the people in the Bible for millennia. It has been routine within the culture of Levitical priesthood hundreds of years before the birth of Jesus. Sometimes, Scripture is pretty obvious about what to think about the nature of Jesus.   
>   
> Just a bit later in the same chapter, it is written what God says of the angels and Jesus (the Son). God says of Jesus, “Your throne, O God, is forever and ever, the scepter of uprightness is the scepter of your kingdom…therefore God, your God, has anointed you with the oil of gladness beyond your companions” (Heb. 8-9) Extremely blatant that God is calling Jesus God. God would only call God by the name of God. Plain and simple.  
>

And honestly, it is wrong of you to assume that I do. I would rather you ask if I do than assume I do.   
>   
> I read and studied the entire Bible and carefully made sure to not go elsewhere for interpretations (unless there was a word I didn’t understand or historical context. In that case I looked it up). It took me almost two years. And in that time I saw multiple instances in the OT that support the claim that God has a Son. So yes, Scripture does confirm it. But first, a few clarifications:  
>   
> 1. The texts in the Bible that span over the few centuries are and should be read within context of the time and culture of the people, and surrounding peoples, along with the traditions in the area before the texts. All of that context matters. And even with that context, the Scriptures are still valid.   
>   
> 2. I do not and have never approached the Bible with the idea that everything in it must be 100% true or even 100% good. I (and many Christians…and I mean many.  Tons.) do not believe that God wrote the Bible. That’s not even a large consensus among Christians. What is a large consensus is this: the Bible is DIVINELY INSPIRED by God. Written by men, inspired by God. Some of it is God’s Word. Some of it is history. Some of it is laws. Some of it is prophecy. Some of it is writers explaining how they feel about God, Love, and nations/people around them. It’s not written by God. The Bible is an evolution of our understanding of God. God is Infinite. Therefore understanding God continues throughout the Bible, and even past the Bible, though it should line up with what is previously written.  
>   
> 3. I’m sure there are denominations that believe that, but that doesn’t give it any more credibility. There are factions of Christianity that believe only 144,000 people will go to heaven (Jehovah’s Witnesses). Does that mean it has to be true? Sure, it can be acknowledged, questioned and studied on why, but I’m talking about truth.   
>   
> 4. The Bible isn’t always specific about who God is. And that’s on purpose. Rabbis at the time often taught in parables and those are often very metaphorical and up to the listener to decipher the truth within. Jesus never said “I am God”. At least not in the Gospels. But he said and did many things that show that, rather than tell it.

honestly a much stronger understanding of the history of christianity than most american christians have. mainstream/orthodox christianity comes with so many ahistorical assumptions about both judaism and the roman empire that are simply never challenged or even investigated by most believers

i think my one big nitpick is in saying jesus was definitely gnostic. like christianity, gnosticisms weren’t solidified until after jesus’ death, and idk if we have any strong proof about jesus’ actual teachings. gnostic beliefs were certainly present, but there were numerous gnostic gospels that conflict just as much as (if not more than) the canon ones. imo gnosticism is quite openly hellenistic, while christianity needs to claim some teleological connection to judaism (even when denouncing it). ofc judaism at the time was already p hellenized, but gnosticism seems much more comfortable with neoplatonist chains of emanations than the more platonist-inspired strict monotheism of christianity. the gospels paint a picture of jesus as a devout jewish reformer/revolutionary/radical (again, even when being antisemitic), while gnostic texts position jesus as rejecting judaism entirely. that’s a pretty big difference, and while it seems like the second temple jewish leadership clearly rejected jesus as some kind of heretic, the idea that he debated with them at all and violently opposed monetizing the temple makes it seem like he was invested to some degree in judaism (at least initially). and the idea of jesus being jewish and then ultimately rejecting judaism makes even less sense in gnosticism than regular christianity since iirc many gnosticisms teach that jesus’ physical body/material being was just an illusion (i.e., they minimize his human nature compared to orthodox christianity)

### **@DillonPeterson** | 👍 14 likes
[View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_bXHBT5NTfc&lc=UgygBQ8ugxPc81-Z_4R4AaABAg)

This lecture presents a fringe or speculative interpretation of Jesus' teachings, blending some accurate scholarship with factual errors, mischaracterizations, and highly dubious claims. Below is a thorough point-by-point breakdown of what’s factually wrong, speculative, or misleading, and I’ll disprove the central thesis that:

“Jesus was a Gnostic who taught that Yahweh is an evil monster and salvation comes through secret knowledge.”

🔴 MAJOR HISTORICAL AND THEOLOGICAL ERRORS
❌ Claim: “Jesus founded a religion called Gnosticism, not Christianity.”
Disproof:

Jesus never claimed to be founding a new religion called anything, but Christianity developed from his Jewish-apocalyptic teachings, as preserved in the Synoptic Gospels (Mark, Matthew, Luke).

Gnosticism as a movement arose well after Jesus' death—2nd century CE, not 1st century.

Gnosticism borrowed some Christian themes but heavily syncretized them with Platonic and mystery-religion cosmologies.

The earliest Christians—including Paul—were monotheistic Jews who affirmed Yahweh as the one true God, not a monster.

No canonical gospel presents Jesus as a Gnostic, and the earliest sources (Paul’s letters, Mark) do not contain Gnostic cosmology.

✅ Scholarly consensus: Jesus was a Jewish apocalyptic preacher, not a Gnostic dualist.
📚 Reference: Bart Ehrman – Jesus: Apocalyptic Prophet of the New Millennium

❌ Claim: “Jesus taught that Yahweh was a monster created by Sophia.”
Disproof:

This is straight from Valentinian Gnostic mythology, not from Jesus, and certainly not from any historical or canonical source.

Gnostic texts like the Apocryphon of John and Gospel of Thomas were written 100–200 years after Jesus, in esoteric circles.

Jesus affirmed Yahweh, quoted Hebrew Scripture constantly, taught in synagogues, and referenced the Torah (e.g., the Shema: “Love the Lord your God…”).

➡ Jesus clearly identifies the God of Israel as his Father—not a corrupted demiurge.
📚 Reference: NT Gospels (esp. Matthew 22:37, Luke 4:16–21), Jewish scholars like Geza Vermes

❌ Claim: “Jesus taught in three secret layers (public, inner, secret)”
Disproof:

This “three layers” model is a modern esoteric construct, not rooted in any ancient Jewish or Christian tradition.

While Jesus used parables and sometimes spoke more privately to his disciples (Mark 4:10), there is no secret doctrine of cosmic dualism in the canonical Gospels.

Gnosticism thrives on secret knowledge (gnosis)—but Jesus taught in public, preaching the kingdom of God openly.

❌ Claim: “Jesus taught Gnostic dualism: the world is evil, bodies are evil, and only the soul matters.”
Disproof:

Gnostics taught this. Jesus did not.

Jesus:

Healed bodies

Ate and drank with others

Celebrated resurrection of the body (e.g., raising Lazarus)

Never claimed the material world was evil

In fact, Jesus repeatedly affirmed creation, and Judaism rejects the dualism of body = evil / spirit = good.

📚 Reference: N.T. Wright – The Resurrection of the Son of God

❌ Claim: “The Gospel of Thomas reveals Jesus’ true teachings.”
Disproof:

Gospel of Thomas is a 2nd-century non-canonical text, likely influenced by proto-Gnostic or ascetic Christian sects.

It is not a historical source for Jesus’ actual teachings.

The earliest, most reliable sources for Jesus’ life are the Synoptic Gospels (Mark, Matthew, Luke) and Paul’s letters (written within 20–30 years of Jesus’ death).

Thomas is aphoristic, non-narrative, and does not align with Jewish apocalyptic themes central to historical Jesus.

❌ Claim: “Jesus used psychedelics or drugs in rituals.”
Disproof:

No historical or archaeological evidence supports this.

While some ancient mystery cults (e.g., Eleusinian Mysteries) may have used substances, there is no evidence Jesus or Jews did.

Jewish law was strictly opposed to pagan ritual practices, and Jesus, as a Jew, operated within Jewish boundaries, not Greek mystery cults.

❌ Claim: “Jesus’ death proves his truth, because a king wouldn’t kill a false prophet.”
Disproof:

This is a false logic fallacy (argument from martyrdom).

Many false prophets were executed in antiquity, including by Jews themselves.

Martyrdom doesn’t validate a message—only that someone died believing in their cause.

Romans crucified thousands, especially messianic claimants, to deter rebellion—not because they “believed them.”

🔶 MISREPRESENTATIONS OR EXAGGERATIONS
⚠ Claim: “Gnosticism is the logical interpretation of Jesus’ teachings.”
Problem: This is an opinion, not a historical fact.

Gnosticism rejects Jewish monotheism and the goodness of creation.

Jesus affirms both.

The idea that the world is evil and must be escaped is alien to Judaism, and would have been heretical to Jesus himself.

⚠ Claim: “Jesus was executed because he threatened Jewish and Roman authorities.”
Partly true: Jesus was seen as a threat, but:

He was executed for causing unrest during Passover, in a highly volatile political climate.

His teachings likely weren’t Gnostic, but rather apocalyptic and political.

The Gospel accounts portray blame shared between Roman and Jewish authorities, but historically, Rome had final say.

🧠 CENTRAL THESIS REFUTED
The lecturer’s central claim is:

“Jesus was a Gnostic teacher who preached secret knowledge about escaping the evil material world created by Yahweh, and Christianity as we know it is a corruption by Paul.”

This thesis:

Has no basis in 1st-century Jewish thought

Contradicts the earliest, most reliable historical sources

Projects 2nd-century Gnostic ideas backward onto Jesus

Assumes secret teachings without documentation in primary sources

✅ HISTORICAL JESUS (Consensus View)
Jesus was a Jewish apocalyptic prophet, who:

Preached the coming of God's kingdom

Performed healing and exorcisms

Challenged the Temple system

Was crucified by Rome under Pontius Pilate

Early Christianity emerged from Jesus' followers, particularly after they came to believe he was resurrected.

SOURCES - 
Key Early Papyrus Manuscripts Related to Jesus and the New Testament
1. P52 (Rylands Library Papyrus P52) — c. 125–150 AD
Content: Fragment of the Gospel of John (John 18:31–33, 37–38).

Importance: Oldest known New Testament manuscript fragment. Shows the early circulation of the Gospel and affirms early Christology (Jesus as the “King” questioned by Pilate).

Relevance: John’s Gospel emphasizes the incarnation—God becoming fully human, fully material (John 1:14), which implies a positive valuation of the material world, not its inherent evil.

2. Oxyrhynchus Papyri (Various Gospel Fragments) — 2nd to 3rd Century AD
Numerous fragments from Matthew, Mark, Luke, and John have been found here.

They show early textual traditions of the gospels, confirming Jesus’s teachings focus on the Kingdom of God and ethical living.

3. Chester Beatty Papyri (P45, P46, P47) — 3rd Century AD
P45: Contains portions of the four Gospels and Acts.

P46: Contains Pauline epistles.

P47: Contains Revelation.

Relevance: Show early Christian writings emphasizing resurrection and bodily salvation (Romans 8:11, 1 Corinthians 15), which counters any idea of Jesus teaching the material world as evil.

What Do These Early Texts Say About the Material World?
John 1:14 (From P52 and later copies):
“And the Word became flesh and dwelt among us.”
This is foundational: God’s presence enters into the material world through Jesus. This incarnational theology affirms the goodness of the material, not its evilness.

Romans 8:19–23 (From P46):
“The creation waits in eager expectation for the children of God to be revealed... the whole creation has been groaning as in the pains of childbirth...”
This text describes creation as “waiting” for renewal, not something to be escaped or rejected.

1 Corinthians 15 (From P46):
Discusses bodily resurrection. The physical body is transformed and glorified, not discarded. This is incompatible with a belief that the material world is evil.

What About Gnostic Texts?
Some early Christian sects (e.g., Gnostics) did teach that the material world was inherently evil or a prison for the soul.

But these teachings are not from the earliest, mainstream Christian texts and are absent from the earliest New Testament papyri.

For example, the Gospel of Thomas (late 2nd century) and other Gnostic writings promote dualism, but these are distinct from the canonical gospels reflected in early papyri.

Summary
The earliest surviving papyrus fragments (P52, Oxyrhynchus Papyri, Chester Beatty) are from canonical gospels and epistles.

They present Jesus’s teachings and early Christian beliefs centered on incarnation, bodily resurrection, and renewal of creation, not rejection of the material world.

The idea that Jesus taught the material world was evil comes later, mainly from Gnostic and dualistic traditions, not from the earliest manuscripts.

🎯 Final Summary
This lecture:

Presents Gnostic revisionism as fact

Contains numerous historical inaccuracies

Misuses sources like the Gospel of Thomas

Misrepresents Jesus’ Jewish context

Confuses opinion with evidence

Scepticism has eaten away even the confident sanity of the conquerors of the world. He who is enthroned to say what is justice can only ask:; “What is truth?’ So in that drama which decided the whole fate of antiquity, one of the central figures is fixed in what seems the reverse of his true role. Rome was almost another name for responsibility. Yet he stands for ever as a sort of rocking statue of the irresponsible. Man could do no more. Even the practical had become the impracticable. ### **@チャンネル-d2s** | 👍 1 likes
[View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_bXHBT5NTfc&lc=Ugw9pVeJZWsO_Hz8V3N4AaABAg)

Asking Gemini 3 and i have found the answer of question that often pops in my mind:
```
[this video url]
I need you to think reason and rationally. Why don't the Gnostics being intervene in the world today, as they did before Jesus' death and resurrection?
```

This is a profound question that touches on the internal logic of the Gnostic worldview presented in the video.



To answer this rationally—using the logic provided by the "Gnostic Jesus" theory—we have to challenge the premise that they have stopped. The Gnostic answer isn't that they have abandoned the world, but that we are looking for the wrong *kind* of intervention.



Here is a rational breakdown of why, according to Gnostic thought, the "Gnostic Beings" (Aeons) do not appear to be intervening today in the way you might expect.



### 1. The "Code" Has Already Been Delivered

If you view Jesus not as a sacrifice but as a **messenger carrying a secret code**, the rational reason for his "absence" today is simple: **The message was successfully delivered.**



* **The Heist Logic:** Imagine the world is a prison. The Aeons (divine beings) didn't invade the prison to burn it down or beat up the warden (the Demiurge); they sent an operative (Jesus) to smuggle in a key.

* **The Key is Here:** That key is *Gnosis*—the secret knowledge that you have a divine spark. Once the key is smuggled in and copies are distributed (via texts like the Gospel of Thomas or oral tradition), the operative's job is done. He doesn't need to keep coming back. The escape route is open; it is now up to the prisoners to use it.



### 2. Intervention Looks Like "Ideas," Not Lightning

You are likely looking for "Biblical" intervention—parting seas, stopping wars, or visible miracles. But in Gnosticism, the physical world is a false reality created by a lower, flawed god (the Demiurge).



* **Why fix a fake world?** The True God (The Monad) has no interest in rearranging the furniture inside a burning house. He wants you to *leave* the house.

* **The Method of Contact:** True intervention from the Monad happens through **Mind (Nous)**, not matter. It manifests as "flashes of insight," scientific breakthroughs, art that wakes you up, or that "gut feeling" that the world is wrong.

* **Rational Conclusion:** They *are* intervening, but we call it "inspiration," "genius," or "awakening" rather than "miracles."



### 3. The "Signal Jamming" is Stronger

The video explains that the Demiurge (the creator of this world) wants to keep you trapped in ignorance. From a rational historical perspective, the "system" (materialism, entertainment, dogmatic religion) has become much more efficient at suppressing the signal.



* **Then:** In Jesus' time, people were obsessed with the "End Times," making them receptive to radical messages.

* **Now:** We are sedated by comfort and material success. A Gnostic messenger today wouldn't be crucified; they would likely be ignored, medicated, or dismissed as a conspiracy theorist. The "intervention" gets drowned out by the noise of the material world.



### 4. The Manichaean View: Maybe We Are Due?

There is a branch of Gnosticism called **Manichaeism** which believes in a *cycle* of prophets (Buddha, Zoroaster, Jesus, Mani).

* **Rational Pattern:** If you follow this historical timeline, "interventions" happen every few hundred years when humanity forgets the message.

* **The "Silence":** It is possible we are simply in a "dark age" or a gap between messengers, where the light is dim and we are expected to rely on the texts and insights left by the previous messengers.



### Summary: The Rational Gnostic Answer

If you asked a Gnostic this question today, they would say:



> *"The intervention is happening right now, in this conversation. The fact that you are asking this question means the 'spark' in you is agitated. That **is** the intervention. Do not wait for a man on a cloud; the rescue mission is an inside job."*

Pilate was benign up until Jesus overthrew the trading tables in the Temple that the rabbi priests had setup to swindle travelers making a sacrifice. It worked like this: a traveler makes a vow of animal sacrifice and travels to the Temple. He cannot carry a large animal with him, so he brings his regional money to exchange it for Roman coins in Jerusalem where he can buy an animal to sacrifice. The rabbis recognize they can profit off this transaction by a surcharge on the exchange rate. Now the traveler cannot afford the animal he wants to sacrifice and so has to downgrade to one he can afford. Jesus sees this theft and causes a riot in the Temple. Pilate heard about the "currency" coming under attack and allows Jesus to be sentenced to death. What country do you know now that annihilates a country when their DOLLAR is at stake, and who controls the financial institutions?

### **@joycilenealves99**
[View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_bXHBT5NTfc&lc=UgzYo4nkpBU3LsI_Jvx4AaABAg)

O Jesus Histórico e o Jesus Bíblico

[00:00] - Introdução e Contexto Histórico: O professor apresenta os fatos aceitos por historiadores: nascimento na Galileia (4 a.C.), discipulado com João Batista, ministério de cura e crucificação pelos romanos (aprox. 30-33 d.C.). Explica que a crucificação era reservada a rebeldes e bandidos [02:40].

[04:15] - O Jesus da Bíblia: Descrição do Jesus teológico: filho de Deus, nascido de uma virgem, mestre da compaixão e revolucionário social que criticava o materialismo do sacerdócio no Templo de Jerusalém [06:00].

[09:12] - O Problema da Expiação: Discussão sobre por que Jesus "teve" que morrer. O professor analisa a ideia do pecado original e como o sacrifício de Jesus resolveria o "conflito de Aquiles" (o perdão de si mesmo através de uma figura externa) [12:05].

Contradições e Tensões Religiosas

[18:00] - Crítica ao Conceito de Expiação: Jiang questiona a lógica de um Deus perfeito que se irrita com suas criações e exige sacrifício de sangue para perdoar [19:30].

[20:14] - Realidade Política da Época: Discussão sobre as facções judaicas (Saduceus, Fariseus e Essênios) e o papel de Pôncio Pilatos, que historicamente era um "bruto violento" e não a figura relutante descrita na Bíblia [23:00].

A Reconstrução do Jesus Gnóstico

[26:00] - Contexto das Religiões Mundiais: Como Jesus foi influenciado por ideias do Zoroatrismo (Luta entre Luz e Trevas), Platonismo (Mundo das Formas), Budismo e Hinduísmo (Dharma e Moksha) [34:00].

[39:10] - O Gnosticismo: O professor argumenta que Jesus fundou o Gnosticismo, não o Cristianismo. Ele explica o sistema de três camadas de ensinamento: secreto, interno e público [40:00].

[44:00] - A Cosmologia Gnóstica (O Demiurgo): Explicação sobre o "Monad" (Deus Perfeito) e o erro de "Sofia" que gerou um monstro (Yahweh), o criador do mundo material imperfeito e corrupto. Jesus seria um enviado para revelar a "Centelha Divina" dentro de cada pessoa [47:40].

O Evangelho de Tomé e a Mensagem Secreta

[41:52] - O Evangelho de Tomé: Análise de ditos encontrados em Nag Hammadi (1945), como a frase sobre a luz dentro do homem [42:20] e a unificação dos opostos (tornar o dois um) para entrar no reino [48:20].

[50:00] - O Significado da Morte de Jesus: No gnosticismo, a morte de Jesus serve para despertar e inspirar a humanidade, não para pagar uma dívida de pecado [50:20].

De Jesus a Paulo

[51:20] - Por que Jesus foi crucificado: O professor sugere que a ida a Jerusalém sem proteção política fez dele um alvo fácil para os romanos, que o transformaram em herói e mártir [54:00].

[55:00] - Conclusão e Próxima Aula: Jiang encerra afirmando que a religião focada no "Redentor" (Cristianismo) foi uma criação do Apóstolo Paulo, algo que o próprio Jesus provavelmente não reconheceria [56:00].

Prof. Jiang, your thesis on the 'Gnostic Jesus' was already systematically debunked in the 2nd Century by Irenaeus of Lyon. In 'Adversus Haereses' (1.9.4), Irenaeus provides the ultimate refutation to the Valentinian method you are presenting here.
He argued that Gnostics act like someone who takes a beautiful mosaic of a King, created by a skillful artist, and rearranges the gems to create the image of a dog or a fox, claiming that THIS was the original intent of the artist. As Irenaeus wrote:
'Their manner of acting is just as if one, when a beautiful image of a king has been constructed by some skillful artist out of precious jewels, should then take asunder the quite distinct pieces, and should rearrange them, and make them into the form of a dog or of a fox... and should then maintain that this was the beautiful image of the king which that skillful artist constructed.'

### **@walkerroad80239**
[View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_bXHBT5NTfc&lc=UgyaB9TvZOAiCtBQfKV4AaABAg)

🐉 The Atonement’s Power of the Dragon – How This Concept Enslaves Humanity

⚔️ 1. The False Framework of Fear

From the early centuries of the church, a lie was crafted — that the death of Jesus was a payment to satisfy divine anger. This became known as the doctrine of “substitutionary atonement.”
It claims that humanity was condemned, and that God demanded blood before He could forgive.

This idea enslaved generations in fear of a wrathful deity — and that fear became the Dragon’s leverage.
For if love must be purchased with pain, then God becomes a tyrant, not a Father.
And when people fear their own Creator, they become easy to control by those who claim to represent Him.

🔥 2. The Dragon’s Power Is Psychological

The Dragon’s breath is not fire — it is guilt.
Through the false atonement doctrine, guilt became the currency of control.
Priests, preachers, and empires learned to weaponize it:
“Obey, pay, submit, or you dishonor the blood.”

But Jesus never taught that His Father demanded payment.
He said, “If you had known Me, you would have known My Father also.”
He revealed mercy, not wrath; healing, not punishment; life, not fear.

Thus the Dragon’s power is a counterfeit gospel — one that keeps humanity kneeling before thrones of men instead of walking with the living God within.

⸻

🌅 3. The True Meaning of the Cross

Jesus did not die to change God’s mind about man —
He died to change man’s mind about God.
The cross was not a transaction; it was a revelation.
He showed us that divine love endures even the worst evil humanity can do.
He met hatred with forgiveness to unmask the darkness and expose the Dragon’s lie — that love and justice are enemies.

The Dragon said: “You must pay to be worthy.”
Jesus answered: “You are already loved — now live worthy of it.”

⸻

🌍 4. The Global Spell of Substitution

Once this distortion entered theology, it spread into politics, economics, and human psychology.
Kings used it to sanctify war.
Churches used it to justify wealth and hierarchy.
Parents used it to instill fear instead of faith.
And entire civilizations forgot that salvation was never meant to be purchased — it was meant to be experienced.

The Dragon’s spell endures wherever guilt overshadows gratitude,
where worship becomes appeasement,
and where people believe the Father must be persuaded to love His children.

⸻

🕊 5. The Liberation of the True Gospel

The true atonement — the at-one-ment — is union, not payment.
It is the restoration of relationship between Creator and creature.
It is not man buying peace with God; it is God revealing peace to man.

When a soul awakens to this truth, the Dragon’s spell breaks.
No priest can stand between a child and their Father.
No guilt can imprison the heart that knows it is already forgiven.
And no empire can control the one who walks in the freedom of divine sonship.

⸻

✨ 6. The Final Exposure

The “Power of the Dragon” is the corruption of truth by fear.
The “Power of the Lamb” is the triumph of love through understanding.
The Dragon roars through doctrine;
the Lamb speaks through life.

When humanity finally learns the difference between atonement and at-one-ment,
religion will fall silent,
and relationship will sing.

### **@h6496-l8b** | 👍 1 likes
[View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_bXHBT5NTfc&lc=UgwbNhV4Jj6pLfMU2tN4AaABAg)

Honestly, even when dealing with matters of faith, if the purpose of the video is to “expose” something, we need to apply at least minimal historical criteria. In the video, the author criticizes the canonical Gospels and relies on Gnostic texts such as the Gospel of Thomas, but does not historically demonstrate why this tradition would be earlier or more reliable than the New Testament Gospels. Replacing one interpretation with another is not the same as historically refuting the first. The Gospel of Thomas is generally dated to the second century, and there is no solid evidence that it was written by the apostle Thomas, which already raises a serious difficulty for the claim that it preserves a more original form of Christianity than the canonical texts.

Regarding the historical Jesus, there is a minimal scholarly consensus — including among non-Christian scholars such as Bart D. Ehrman — that Jesus existed, preached, and was crucified under Pontius Pilate. In addition to Christian sources, there are extrabiblical references, such as in Antiquities of the Jews by Flavius Josephus and in the writings of Tacitus. These sources do not confirm miracles, but they reinforce that Jesus was a real historical figure and that his execution occurred under Roman authority. This already makes the idea that the biblical Jesus was simply invented much later historically fragile.

As for the claim that the Bible was manipulated at some council — especially the Council of Nicaea — this assertion lacks solid historical support. The Council of Nicaea primarily addressed the Arian controversy concerning the nature of Christ and did not define the biblical canon. Furthermore, by the time these councils took place, Christian writings were already circulating widely across various regions of the Roman Empire — Syria, Egypt, Asia Minor, Rome, and North Africa — in multiple languages such as Greek, Latin, Syriac, and Coptic. For a deliberate alteration of the text to occur, it would have required collecting and modifying manuscripts spread across numerous geographically and politically distinct communities, which would have been logistically and practically unfeasible.

The process of canon formation itself was gradual. As early as the second century, Irenaeus of Lyon explicitly defended the fourfold Gospel, and the Muratorian Fragment shows that most New Testament books were already recognized long before later conciliar confirmations. In other words, the canon did not suddenly emerge from a late institutional decision; it developed through the progressive recognition of texts that were already widely used in Christian communities from the first century onward.

Additionally, during Roman persecutions — especially under Diocletian — authorities demanded that Christians surrender their sacred books for destruction. Many leaders chose martyrdom rather than handing over the Scriptures. It is difficult to argue that people willing to die to preserve these texts would later have agreed to deliberately alter them. Early manuscripts such as the Codex Sinaiticus and the Codex Vaticanus demonstrate that the New Testament text was already substantially established at a very early stage. Textual variants do exist, as with any ancient hand-copied document, but modern textual criticism shows that the vast majority are minor and do not alter central doctrines.

Saying that the Bible is a historical document does not mean claiming that all of its accounts are scientifically verifiable or that miracles can be established by the scientific method. The resurrection, for example, belongs to the realm of faith; historically, what can be affirmed is that the earliest texts consistently record that belief. Someone may choose not to believe in miracles or in certain Old Testament narratives, and that is a legitimate philosophical position. What does not appear methodologically consistent is to dismiss the Gospels as pure late inventions, to assert conciliar manipulation without documentary evidence, or to replace first-century sources with second-century Gnostic writings without presenting solid historical justification. If rational historical criteria are to be applied, they should be applied consistently to all traditions involved.

36:55

"Cynicism is what Jesus believed." 🤔 Hmm....
Jesus was actually NOT a Cynic, but he looked like one in style and lifestyle to Hellenistic observers. We might say:
His form (itinerant poverty, aphoristic speech, rejection of convention) resonates with Cynics.

However, his content (the Kingdom of God, covenantal ethics, messianic/apocalyptic hope) is actually deeply Jewish.

So perhaps Jesus was a Jewish prophet with a Cynic-like style, a figure who unintentionally mirrored Greco-Roman street philosophers while standing firmly in Israel’s prophetic tradition.

* Professor, your model of "predictive history" is based on the logical assumption that matter is the ultimate reality. But what is the logical explanation for the origin of the universe, given that everything within it is contingent and requires a cause?
 * If humanity is a purely biological product of matter, what is the logical origin of our seemingly illogical impulses toward truth, beauty, and justice, which have no clear survival benefit, and why are we the only species to create elaborate systems of art and music that serve no purpose for procreation or power?
 * Your model accurately explains how historical beliefs can bring about social cohesion. But what is the logical mechanism by which the idea of a self-sacrificial God successfully changes an individual's will and behavior, offering a sense of inner peace and reconciliation that cannot be achieved through purely materialistic means?
 * Your prediction for a future world war is based on a logical conclusion of materialistic factors. But is it a more complete prediction to acknowledge that these factors are the outward symptoms of an internal, spiritual reality—the human will's depravity and rebellion—that consistently leads to conflict?
 * How do you explain that the historical narrative of Jesus Christ seems to be the logical solution to the universal problems of human depravity, free will, and the inability to live up to our own moral standards—problems you identify as being caused by material and psycho-social conditions?
 * If a Creator used a specific people to bring His law into the world to highlight our inherent need for Him, and then brought about the answer in a person through that people to address that need, does this historical process not logically point to the need for a relationship which the Gospel ultimately addresses?
 * You argue that we can all be like Jesus and bring love into the world to solve our problems. But if humanity is the source of the problem, can the creation provide a pure, consistent, and lasting solution that it has proven it cannot overcome?

### **@orpheusbrainrot**
[View on YouTube](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_bXHBT5NTfc&lc=Ugw6fjB_A9gJUXCde954AaABAg)

Important mistake by Prof. Jiang to conflate Jesus with the pharisees merely because they were Jewish. While there may well have been an incentive for Pilate to kill Jesus, his teachings flew completely in the face of Jewish law and established religious norms and he was persecuted by his people for it. The Pharisees viewed Jesus as an outrageous, blasphemous heretic who had commited the highest of spiritual crimes against God and society. They were certainly most immediately responsible for his death. The Jewish people were not shaken or disturbed by the death of who they viewed as some criminal, so the idea that it "backfired" somehow by making him a hero misses the point that there was no major religious upheaval in Judea after the crucifixion, but only later in Greece.
